\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}(\beta)\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})\{Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},1)\}+\widehat{b}(X_{i},1)-\beta\right]
, and uses linear approximation only to approximate the nuisance residuals
\widehat{\xi}-\xi
and
\widehat{b}-b
. The bias of
\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}(\beta)
has a product form involving two first-step estimation errors:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\{\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}(\beta)\}-\psi(\beta)={\mathbb{E}}[\{\widehat{\xi}(X,T)-\xi(X,T)\}\{b(X,T)-\widehat{b}(X,T)\}].

(S5.6)

Consistent with the main text, we employ sample splitting: the nuisance estimators
\widehat{\xi}
and
\widehat{b}
are obtained from an independent nuisance sample and all expectations
\mathbb{E}[\cdot]
heretofore in this section are taken with respect to the main sample conditional on the nuisance sample. Hence
\widehat{\xi}
and
\widehat{b}
can be treated as fixed functions.

Our framework imposes no linearity restriction on
\xi
and
b
. We allow
\widehat{\xi}
and
\widehat{b}
to be obtained using flexible methods (e.g., kernel smoothing, series estimation, or machine learning), although to obtain sharp theoretical results, we sometimes impose certain smoothness assumptions on
\xi-\widehat{\xi}
and
b-\widehat{b}
such that the truncation bias that cannot be captured by the basis
\bar{\phi}_{k}
is sufficiently small. We project the nuisance approximation residuals
\widehat{\xi}-\xi
and
\widehat{b}-b
onto the sieve space spanned by
\bar{\phi}_{k}(x,t)
.
As described in Section
3.1
, this projection leads to a decomposition of (
S5.6
) into (i) a truncation bias
\mathrm{TB}_{\psi,k}
and (ii) a part of the bias (
S5.6
) captured by the
k
-dimensional dictionary
\bar{\phi}_{k}
. The latter can be written as a bilinear functional

\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}\equiv\alpha_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\eta_{k},

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\xi}(X,T)-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X)],\quad\eta_{k}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[T(Y-\widehat{b}(X,T))\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X)]

are
k
-dimensional vectors; See Section
3.1
and
3.2
for a detailed derivation.

Next, we use the bilinear functional
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}=\alpha_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\eta_{k}
as a concrete example to show that the two constructions based on jackknife bias correction and
U
-statistics lead to the same estimator. Specifically, we take
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
as the parameter of interest and
\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{k}
are high-dimensional nuisance parameters.
We also assume that
\Sigma_{k}\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)^{\otimes 2}]
is known for convenience. Our strategy estimates
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
by leveraging its equivalent bilinear form:

\displaystyle\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}\equiv\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{\xi}(X,T)-1\}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X)]^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[T\{Y-\widehat{b}(X,T)\}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X)],

which can be unbiasedly estimated by the following second-order
U
-statistic:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}\coloneqq{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}[\{\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1\}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}\{Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j})\}].

(S5.7)

The jackknife-based bias correction procedure in
Cattaneo et al. 2019
starts from the plug-in estimator of
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}=\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k},

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{k}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1\}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i}),\quad\widehat{\eta}_{k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}\{Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i})\}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i}).

The estimator
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}
, which is a
V
-statistic, will have a bias of an order greater than or equal to
n^{-1/2}
when
k\gtrsim\sqrt{n}
. To implement the jackknife-based bias correction procedure in
Cattaneo et al. 2019
, for each
\ell\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, we compute the leave-one-out analogs of
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(-\ell)}=\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{(-\ell)\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{(-\ell)},

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{(-\ell)}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})=\frac{n}{n-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}-\frac{1}{n-1}(T_{\ell}\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell}),

(S5.8)

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{(-\ell)}\coloneqq\frac{n}{n-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}-\frac{1}{n-1}T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell}))\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell}).

(S5.9)

Let
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(\cdot)}=n^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(-\ell)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{(-\ell)\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{(-\ell)}
be
the average of the leave-one-out estimators. Then, the jackknife bias-corrected estimator is defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{jackknife}}=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}-(n-1)(\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(\cdot)}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}).

Lemma
6
in the end of this subsection shows that the jackknife bias-corrected estimator coincides exactly with our second-order
U
-statistic estimator, i.e.,

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{jackknife}}=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}.

The above derivation shows that, in the special case where the target functional admits the bilinear representation
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}=\alpha_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\eta_{k}
, the two constructions
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}
and
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{jackknife}}
yield the same
U
-statistic. The key ingredient of our approach is to first construct a representation like
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
and estimate
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
by a natural
U
-statistic. By contrast, the jackknife-based bias correction method of
Cattaneo et al. 2019
can be viewed as a general bias correction scheme for parameters such as
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
, for which standard plug-in estimators (such as the standard
M
-estimation) have non-negligible bias when the dimension is large relative to
\sqrt{n}
. It is worth mentioning that the approach in
Cattaneo et al. 2019
can deal with parameters of a more generic nonlinear form than the bilinear form
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
, but the theoretical results are restricted to
k=O(\sqrt{n})
due to the nonlinearity of the more general problems considered. For
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
, we have seen that the
U
-statistic estimator is unbiased for any
k
when
\Sigma_{k}
is known. Finally, if
\Sigma_{k}
is unknown and is replaced by
\widehat{\Sigma}_{k}
, our Supplementary Material Section
S3
derives HOIFs of
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
to use higher-order
U
-statistics for further bias correction. To our knowledge, systematically extending jackknife-based corrections to control such higher-order remainder terms seems to be beyond the scope of
Cattaneo et al. 2019
, but we suspect that constructions similar to those in HOIFs can be considered. We also refer interested readers to
Lin et al. 2024
for a recent result in this direction.

Finally, we record Lemma
6
and its proof below.

Lemma 6
.

The following algebraic identity holds:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\textrm{jackknife}}=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}.

Proof.

Substituting the explicit leave-one-out formulas for
\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{(-\ell)}
and
\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{(-\ell)}
in (
S5.8
)–(
S5.9
) into the definition of
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(\cdot)}
, we have

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(\cdot)}=\ \frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{(-\ell)\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{(-\ell)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left\{\frac{n}{n-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}-\frac{1}{n-1}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})\right\}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\left\{\frac{n}{n-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}-\frac{1}{n-1}T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell}))\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n^{2}}{(n-1)^{2}}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}-\frac{2n}{(n-1)^{2}}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left\{1-\frac{1}{(n-1)^{2}}\right\}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}+\frac{1}{n(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})).

Then, we obtain

\displaystyle(n-1)(\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(\cdot)}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}+\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})).

Hence, using the above result, we derive

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{jackknife}}=\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}-(n-1)(\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{(\cdot)}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(1+\frac{1}{n-1}\right)\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}-\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n}{(n-1)n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{\ell})T_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\widehat{b}(X_{\ell},T_{\ell}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{(n-1)n}\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k},

which completes the proof.
∎

S5.2
Discussion of
Cattaneo and Jansson 2018

We next discuss how the bootstrap-based bias correction approach developed in
Cattaneo and Jansson 2018
is also connected to our higher-order estimators.

Cattaneo and Jansson 2018
study two-step semiparametric estimation and inference with kernel-based first-step estimators, allowing the first step to converge slowly when the bandwidth is small. To facilitate comparison with our framework, we again use the ATE example in Section
S5.2
, where the target moment
\psi(\beta)
is linear in the nuisance functions
\xi
and
b
. In this linear case, the bias analyzed in
Cattaneo and Jansson 2018
is mainly driven by the leave-in bias that results from the use of the same sample in both steps, when the bandwidth is small. They show that, certain nonparametric bootstrap procedures can also recover the leading bias term and hence deliver valid inference.

Our construction allows the nuisance estimators
\widehat{\xi}
and
\widehat{b}
to be obtained by generic learners, such as random forests, or neural networks. By using sample splitting, we focus on the approximation bias. We first approximate this bias by a bilinear functional
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
induced by a dictionary-based projection, and then estimate this functional directly via a
U
-statistic estimator.

Despite this difference, the two approaches are closely connected. As discussed in Section
S5.2
, once the target is reduced to the bilinear functional
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
, we will show that the resulting bootstrap bias-corrected estimator is asymptotically equivalent to our
U
-statistics-based estimator
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}
.

Let
\{O_{i}=(Y_{i},T_{i},X_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the original sample and let
O_{1}^{*},\ldots,O_{n}^{*}
be a bootstrap sample drawn with replacement from
\{O_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Define

^

α

_k^*=

1

n

∑_j=1^n (
^

ξ

(X_j^*,T_j^*)-1)
¯

z

_k (X_j^*),

^

η

_k^*=

1

n

∑_j=1^n T_j^*(Y_j^*-
^

b

(X_j^*,T_j^*))
¯

z

_k(X_j^*).

The bootstrap plug-in estimator of
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}=\alpha_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\eta_{k}
is

^

B

_
ψ
,k^*=(
^

α

_k^*)^⊤Σ_k^-1
^

η

_k^*.

Let
M_{ni}
be the number of times that
O_{i}
is “redrawn” from
\{O_{1},\ldots,O_{n}\}
to form
O_{1}^{*},\ldots,O_{n}^{*},
. By construction, the vector of counts
(M_{n1},\ldots,M_{nn})
is independent of

O_{1},\ldots,O_{n}
and multinomially distributed with parameters
n
and (probabilities)
1/n,\ldots,1/n
. This representation allows us to rewrite any bootstrap empirical average as a weighted average over the original sample, with random weights
\{M_{ni}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{*}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{j}^{*},T_{j}^{*})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j}^{*})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{ni}(T_{i}\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})

and similarly

\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{*}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{ni}T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i}).

The difference
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{*}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}
provides a natural estimator of the bias of the plug-in estimator
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}
for
\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}
. For simplicy, we define the oracle bootstrap bias corrected estimator as

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{bootstrap}}=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}-{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{*}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}],

(S5.10)

where
{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[]
denotes expectation conditional on the original sample.
We refer to this estimator as oracle because it involves the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[]
. In practice, one can approximate this conditional expectation by the Monte Carlo bootstrap average over
B
resamples.

Lemma
7
below shows that

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{bootstrap}}=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}}\right).

Under
k=o(n^{2})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{k}{n^{2}}\cdot\frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}=\frac{k}{n\sqrt{k+n}}=o(1).

Hence, the remainder term is asymptotically negligible under the normalization used in our distributional results. Therefore,
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{bootstrap}}
and
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}
are asymptotically equivalent up to an error term of order
k/n^{2}
, which is
o(n^{-1/2})
(negligible compared to the parametric rate) if
k=o(n^{3/2})
.

Lemma 7
.

The following hold under Assumptions
6
–
8
:

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{bootstrap}}=\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}}\right).

Proof.

Using the definitions of
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{*}
and
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{*}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}=

\displaystyle\ (\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{*})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}^{*}-\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\{M_{ni}M_{nj}-1\}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\{M_{ni}M_{nj}-1\}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}k(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{M_{ni}^{2}-1\}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i})).

Since

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[M_{ni}M_{nj}]=\frac{n(n-1)}{n^{2}}=1-\frac{1}{n},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[M_{ni}^{2}]=\frac{n(n-1)}{n^{2}}+1=\frac{n-1}{n}+1=2-\frac{1}{n},

taking
{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[\cdot]
conditional on the original sample yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{*}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}]=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i})).

Hence,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{bootstrap}}=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}-{\mathbb{E}}^{*}[\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}^{*}-\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k,\text{plug-in}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n+1}{n}\widehat{\alpha}_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\widehat{\eta}_{k}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n+1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{n+1}{n^{3}}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}-\frac{1}{n^{3}(n-1)}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\psi,k}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}}\right),

where the last equality holds by Lemma
8
.
∎

Lemma 8
.

The following hold under Assumptions
6
–
8
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-1)n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i\neq j,j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j}))=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right).

Proof.

Denote

\displaystyle I\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i\neq j,j=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{j})T_{j}(Y_{j}-\widehat{b}(X_{j},T_{j})),

\displaystyle II\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i})).

For the term
I
, the same variance calculation as in Lemma
15
yields
\mathrm{var}(I)=O(1/n+k/n^{2})
. For the mean,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[I]={\mathbb{E}}[(\widehat{\xi}(X,T)-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X)]^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X)T(Y-\widehat{b}(X,T))]=\alpha_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}\eta_{k}=O\left(1\right),

where the last equality holds by the definitions of
\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{k}
, and the condition that the largest eigenvalue of
\Sigma_{k}
is bounded. Combining mean and variance bounds,

\displaystyle I=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n}+1\right)=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right).

For the term
II
, by
\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(x)^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}}_{k}(x)\leq\|\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(x)\|^{2}\lesssim\zeta_{k}^{2}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\zeta_{k}^{2}}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))\right|\right]=O\left(\frac{\zeta_{k}^{2}}{n}\right)=O\left(\frac{k}{n}\right).

Thus, by Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle II=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\xi}(X_{i},T_{i})-1)\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k}(X_{i})T_{i}(Y_{i}-\widehat{b}(X_{i},T_{i}))=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right).

Combining the results for terms
I
and
II
, we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix S6
Technical Results Related to
U
-Processes

In this section, we establish refined local maximal inequalities for
U
-processes that can be used to establish convergence rates and asymptotic normality of higher-order estimators considered in this paper. These are key technical tools for the main results in Sections
3.3
and
4.2
, and may also be of independent interest for other related applications. We begin by introducing the necessary notation.

S6.1
Notation and Review of Concepts from
U
-Process Theory

We first recall the key notation introduced in Section
1.3
, and then introduce additional terminologies for
U
-statistics and
U
-processes that will be used in the technical lemmas that follow.

Recall that
{\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}
is the
m
-th order
U
-statistic operator, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}h\equiv{\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}h(O_{1},\cdots,O_{m})=\frac{(n-m)!}{n!}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\neq\cdots\neq i_{m}\leq n}h(O_{i_{1}},\cdots,O_{i_{m}})

for any measurable function (kernel)
h:{\mathcal{O}}^{m}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
. So,
{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}\equiv{\mathbb{P}}_{n}
is understood to be the empirical mean operator. Let
S_{m}
denote the symmetrization of a function of
m
variables,

\displaystyle S_{m}h(O_{1},\cdots,O_{m})=\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\neq\cdots\neq i_{m}\leq m}h(O_{i_{1}},\cdots,O_{i_{m}}).

Because a
U
-statistic averages over unordered subsets,
{\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}h={\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}\bigl(S_{m}h\bigr)
.
Accordingly, all subsequent notation and results are stated for
symmetric kernels; when a kernel is asymmetric, they apply by default to
its symmetrized version.

For any symmetric measurable function
h:{\mathcal{O}}^{m}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
j=1,\ldots,m
, let
\mathbb{P}^{m-j}h
denote the function on
{\mathcal{O}}^{j}
defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{P}^{m-j}h(o_{1},...,o_{j})=&\ {\mathbb{E}}[h(o_{1},\ldots,o_{j},O_{j+1},\ldots,O_{m})]\\
=&\int\cdots\int h(o_{1},\ldots,o_{j},o_{j+1},\ldots,o_{m}){\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}(o_{j+1})\cdots{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}(o_{m}),\end{split}

(S6.1)

where we always take
O_{1},\cdots,O_{m}
to be i.i.d. drawn from a common law
{\mathbb{P}}
in this paper. Thus,
\mathbb{P}^{0}h\equiv h
and
\mathbb{P}^{m}h\equiv{\mathbb{E}}[h(O_{1},\ldots,O_{m})]
. We also introduce the following shorthand notation for Hoeffding projection of a symmetric measurable function
h
, for
j\leq m
:

\pi_{j}h(o_{1},\ldots,o_{j})\coloneqq(\delta_{o_{1}}-\mathbb{P})\cdots(\delta_{o_{j}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbb{P}^{m-j}h(o_{1},\ldots,o_{m}).

(S6.2)

For a non-symmetric kernel
h
we work with its symmetrised form

S_{m}h
; all the above definitions are understood with that replacement, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}^{m-j}h\equiv\mathbb{P}^{m-j}S_{m}h
and
\pi_{j}h\equiv\pi_{j}S_{m}h
. Then the Hoeffding decomposition of
{\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}h
of any measurable functions
h
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{n,m}h-\mathbb{P}^{m}h=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\binom{m}{j}{\mathbb{U}}_{n,j}(\pi_{j}h).

We next review some basic terminologies about
U
-processes used throughout.
Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a class of symmetric measurable functions
f:{\mathcal{O}}^{m}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
. We assume that there is a symmetric measurable envelope
\mathrm{F}
for
{\mathcal{F}}
, that is
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}|f|\leq\mathrm{F}
,
such that
\mathbb{P}^{m}\mathrm{F}^{2}<\infty
. We will assume certain uniform covering number conditions for the function class
{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
\|\cdot\|_{{\mathcal{F}}}
denote the supremum norm over
{\mathcal{F}}
, i.e.
\|f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}}\coloneqq\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}|f|
.
For a probability measure
Q
and a constant
p>1
, let
\|\cdot\|_{Q,p}
denote the
L_{p}(Q)
-seminorm, i.e.,
\|f\|_{Q,p}\coloneqq\{\int|f(x)|^{p}{\mathrm{d}}Q(x)\}^{1/p}
for finite q while
\|f\|_{Q,\infty}
denotes the essential supremum of
|f|
with respect to
Q
. For any function class
{\mathcal{F}}
with
\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,p}>0
, we use
\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,p},\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,p})
to denote the minimal number of
\|\cdot\|_{Q,p}
-balls of radius
\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,p}
needed to cover
{\mathcal{F}}
. See Section 2.1 of
(
22
)
for details. For
j=1,\ldots,m
, we define the uniform entropy integral

\displaystyle J_{j}(\delta,{\mathcal{F}},\mathrm{F})\coloneqq\int_{0}^{\delta}\sup_{Q}\left\{1+\log\mathsf{N}(\mathbb{P}^{m-j}{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\tau\|\mathbb{P}^{m-j}\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,2})\right\}^{j/2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau,

where
\mathbb{P}^{m-j}{\mathcal{F}}=\{\mathbb{P}^{m-j}f:f\in{\mathcal{F}}\}
and
\sup_{Q}
is taken over all finitely discrete distributions on
{\mathcal{O}}^{j}
. It is straightforward to verify that
\mathbb{P}^{m-j}\mathrm{F}
is an envelope for
\mathbb{P}^{m-j}{\mathcal{F}}
. To avoid measurability difficulties, we will assume that
\mathrm{F}
is pointwise measurable. If
\mathrm{F}
is pointwise measurable and
\mathbb{P}^{m}\mathrm{F}<\infty
(which we have assumed), then
\pi_{j}{\mathcal{F}}\coloneqq\{\pi_{j}f:f\in{\mathcal{F}}\}
and
\mathbb{P}^{m-j}\mathrm{F}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
are all pointwise measurable by the dominated convergence theorem.

For completeness, we recall Definitions
1
and
2
.

Definition 1

(VC-subgraph class)
.

A collection
{\mathcal{F}}
of measurable functions is called a VC-subgraph class, or a VC-class, if the collection of all subgraphs of the functions (see Section 2.6.2 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 2023
) in
{\mathcal{F}}
forms a VC-class of sets (see Section 2.6.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 2023
).

Definition 2

(VC type class)
.

A function class
{\mathcal{F}}
with envelope
\mathrm{F}
is said to be of VC type with characteristic
(A,v)
if
\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}\left({\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,2}\right)\leq(A/\epsilon)^{v}
for all
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, where
\sup_{Q}
denotes the supremum over all finitely discrete probability measures.

S6.2
Local Maximal Inequalities for
U
-Processes

In this section we allow the function class to depend explicitly on the sample size
n
. Throughout, we denote a generic function class by
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}

to emphasize this dependence and derive non-asymptotic bounds that hold for each fixed
n
.

(
11
)
establish local maximal inequalities for
U
-processes indexed by a VC-type class
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\{f:{\mathcal{O}}^{2}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\}
, whose envelope is denoted by
\mathrm{F}_{n}
. Their bounds are expressed in terms of the VC characteristics
(A_{n},v_{n})
and quantities including
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2-k}f\|_{\mathbb{P}^{k},2}
and
\|\mathbb{P}^{2-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{k},2}
for
k=1,2
(see their Corollary 5.5).
Because their bound uses the projected envelope
\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)
(i.e. the conditional mean of
\mathrm{F}_{n}
in one of the arguments), it can be unnecessarily loose when
\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}
is of greater magnitude in order than the quantity of actual interest,
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}|\mathbb{P}f|
. To sharpen the analysis, we work directly with
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}|\mathbb{P}f|
and derive a refined maximal inequality tailored specifically for our problem. The next lemma records this result; as indicated, it is particularly useful when
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}|\mathbb{P}f|
is much less than
\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}
.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose that (1)
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\{f:{\mathcal{O}}^{2}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\}
is a collection of symmetric pointwise measurable functions from
{\mathcal{O}}^{2}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
with envelope function
\mathrm{F}_{n}
, and (2)
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is of VC type with characteristics
(A_{n},v_{n})
with
A_{n}\geq e
and
v_{n}\geq 1
(recall from Definition
1
). Then

(i)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{2}f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim\frac{v_{n}\log A_{n}\cdot\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}}{n}+\frac{(v_{n}\log A_{n})^{1/2}\cdot\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(ii)

If we further assume (3)
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq m_{n}
, and
\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{2}f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\left(\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}+m_{n}\right)\cdot v_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}{n}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}}\,,

where
A_{n}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sqrt{A_{n}}M_{n}/m_{n}
.

Remark 14
.

For the class
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}={\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}
defined in (
S7.8
) in Section
S7.1
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is of VC type with fixed characteristics
(4A,v)
and envelope
\mathrm{F}_{n}=\mathrm{H}_{2,n}
. In this case, the following hold:
\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\leq\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}\lesssim\sqrt{k}
,
\|\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim k
,
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|f\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}\lesssim\sqrt{k}
with
k\lesssim n^{2}
. Moreover, because each
\mathbb{P}f(\cdot)
is a conditional expectation (recall from the notation (
S6.1
)), (
S7.1
) implies
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
and
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\lesssim~1
. Plugging these bounds into Corollary 5.5 of
(
11
)
renders all higher–order projection terms negligible, leaving the first order term:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{2}f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\sqrt{v\log(A\vee n)}+\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot v\log(A\vee n)}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\sqrt{k}\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\,.

Instead, our maximal inequality replaces the projected envelope
\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}
by the tighter quantity

\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}|\mathbb{P}f|
, and
\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}
only influences the bound through the VC characteristics. Thus,
\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}
only appears in a logarithmic factor, which in turn is dominated by
\log n
. Specifically, Lemma
9
(ii) implies that:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{2}f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{k}\log(n)}{n}\,.

Even when
n\lesssim k\lesssim n^{2}
, the right–hand side of the above display still shrinks to zero. Hence, the refined inequality remains informative
in the regime where the bound by directly applying results in
(
11
)
is not sufficiently sharp.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

For any
f
defined on
{\mathcal{O}}^{2}
, we have the following decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{2}f={\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}\left(\pi_{2}f\right)+2{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}\left(\pi_{1}f\right).

Here,
\pi_{1}
is the Hajék projections defined in Section
S6.1
.
Since the function class
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is of VC type with characteristics
(A_{n},v_{n})
satisfying
A_{n}\geq e
and
v_{n}\geq 1
,
applying Corollary 5.6 of
Chen and Kato 2020
to function class
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
with their
k=1,2
and
p=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(\pi_{2}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{J_{2}(1,{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\cdot\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}}{n}\lesssim\frac{v_{n}\log A_{n}\cdot\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}}{n},

(S6.3)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}(\pi_{1}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{J_{1}(1,{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\cdot\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}}{\sqrt{n}}\lesssim\frac{(v_{n}\log A_{n})^{1/2}\cdot\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(S6.4)

Combining (
S6.3
)–(
S6.4
), we prove part (i) of this lemma.

If we further assume condition (3) stated in this lemma, we can sharpen the bound for the first–order
term
{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}\left(\pi_{1}f\right)
. Define the function class
\pi_{1}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\{f=\pi_{1}f:f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\}
. For any
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
, by definition of
\pi_{1}
, we have
\pi_{1}f=\mathbb{P}f-\mathbb{P}^{2}f
. Up to a universal constant, centering does not alter the VC-type characteristics. Consequently, using Lemma
14
, we obtain
\pi_{1}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is still of VC type with characteristics of order
(4\sqrt{A_{n}}M_{n}/m_{n},2v_{n})
and envelope
m_{n}
. Since
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}/m_{n}\leq 1
, applying Lemma
13
to the function class
\pi_{1}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}(f)\right\|_{\pi_{1}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim\frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{m_{n}v_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}{n},

(S6.5)

where
A_{n}^{\prime}=\sqrt{A_{n}}M_{n}/m_{n}
.
Since
A_{n}^{\prime}\geq A_{n}
, combining (
S6.3
) and (
S6.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{2}f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(\pi_{2}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}(\pi_{1}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\left(\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}+m_{n}\right)\cdot v_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}{n}+\frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}},

which completes the proof.
∎

The function class encountered in this paper generally has a product structure
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}=\bigl\{\,f\cdot g_{n}:f\in{\mathcal{F}}\bigr\}
,
where
{\mathcal{F}}
is of a fixed VC-type class with characteristics

(A,v)
, and

g_{n}
is a single function that depends on
n
. By Lemma
12
below,

{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
is again of a VC-type class whose characteristics remain independent of
n
, whereas its envelope,
\mathrm{F}_{n}=\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}|f|\cdot|g_{n}|
, can vary with
n
. For such a function class, we develop a new and readily useful maximal inequality for third-order
U
-processes, which are heavily used in our higher-order estimators. The proof parallels that of Lemma
9
, with additional steps to handle the third-order Hoeffding projection term
{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(\pi_{3}f)
.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose that (1)
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\{f:{\mathcal{O}}^{3}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\}
is a collection of symmetric functions from
{\mathcal{O}}^{3}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
with envelope function
\mathrm{F}_{n}
, (2)
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is of VC type with bounded characteristics
(A,v)
(recall from Definition
1
), and further (3)
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2}f(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq m_{n}
, and
\|\mathbb{P}^{2}\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{3}f\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim\frac{\left(\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}+m_{n}\right)\cdot\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}{n}+\frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}},

where
A_{n}^{\prime}\coloneqq M_{n}/m_{n}
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
9
, we first apply the Hoeffding decomposition:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(f)-\mathbb{P}^{3}f={\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(\pi_{3}f\right)+3{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}\left(\pi_{2}f\right)+3{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}\left(\pi_{1}f\right),

where
\pi_{j}
for
j=1,2,3
represent the Hoeffding projections as defined in Section
S6.1
. By Jensen’s inequality, we have
\|\mathbb{P}^{3-2}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}=O(\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2})
.
Since the function class
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is of VC type with bounded characteristics
(A,v)
independent of the sample size
n
, we have
J_{3}(1,{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\lesssim 1
and
J_{2}(1,{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\lesssim 1
. Thus,
applying Corollary 5.6 of
Chen and Kato 2020
to function class
{\mathcal{F}}
with their
k=2,3
and
p=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(\pi_{3}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim\frac{J_{3}(1,{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\cdot\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}}{n^{3/2}}\lesssim\frac{\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}}{n^{3/2}}

(S6.6)

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(\pi_{2}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim\frac{J_{2}(1,{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\cdot\|\mathbb{P}^{3-1}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}}{n}\lesssim\frac{\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}}{n}.

(S6.7)

Following the same analysis for the empirical process
\pi_{1}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\{f=\pi_{1}f:f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\}
as in Lemma
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}(f)\right\|_{\pi_{1}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{m_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}{n},

(S6.8)

where
A_{n}^{\prime}=M_{n}/m_{n}
.

Combining (
S6.6
)–(
S6.8
), we conclude

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(f)\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(\pi_{3}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(\pi_{2}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}(\pi_{1}f)\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}+m_{n}\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}{n}+\frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(A_{n}^{\prime}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}},

which completes the proof.
∎

We next quote a result from Theorem 3 of
Andrews 1994
that will be useful in the proof.

Lemma 11

(Uniform entropy for sums and products)
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}
be two classes of functions with envelopes
\mathrm{F}
and
\mathrm{G}
, respectively. Then, for any
\epsilon>0
, the uniform entropy numbers of
{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}=\{fg:f\in{\mathcal{F}},g\in{\mathcal{G}}\}
and
{\mathcal{F}}+{\mathcal{G}}=\{f+g:f\in{\mathcal{F}},g\in{\mathcal{G}}\}
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{F}}+{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},2\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}+\mathrm{G}\|_{Q,2})\leq\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,2})\cdot\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathrm{G}\|_{Q,2}),

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{F}}\cdot{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},2\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}\cdot\mathrm{G}\|_{Q,2})\leq\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,2},)\cdot\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathrm{G}\|_{Q,2}).

Lemma 12

(VC–type characteristics of sums and products)
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
are pointwise measurable measurable and VC type with characteristics
(A_{{\mathcal{F}}},v_{{\mathcal{F}}})
and
(A_{{\mathcal{G}}},v_{{\mathcal{G}}})

, and envelopes
\mathrm{F}
and
\mathrm{G}
, respectively. Then,
{\mathcal{F}}+{\mathcal{G}}=\{f+g:f\in{\mathcal{F}},g\in{\mathcal{G}}\}
and
{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}=\{fg:f\in{\mathcal{F}},g\in{\mathcal{G}}\}
are VC type with characteristics
A^{\prime}\coloneqq 2(A_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{v_{{\mathcal{F}}}}A_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{v_{{\mathcal{G}}}})^{1/v^{\prime}}
and
v^{\prime}\coloneqq v_{{\mathcal{F}}}+v_{{\mathcal{G}}}
. Specially, when
{\mathcal{G}}=\{g\}
,
{\mathcal{F}}+{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
will be VC type with characteristics
(2A_{{\mathcal{F}}},v_{{\mathcal{F}}})
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
11
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}\bigl(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G},\,\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\,\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}+\mathrm{G}\|_{Q,2}\bigr)\leq

\displaystyle\ \sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}\bigl(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon/2\|\mathrm{F}\|_{Q,2}\bigr)\;\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}\bigl(\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon/2\|\mathrm{G}\|_{Q,2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \left(\frac{2A_{{\mathcal{F}}}}{\epsilon}\right)^{v_{{\mathcal{F}}}}\left(\frac{2A_{{\mathcal{G}}}}{\epsilon}\right)^{v_{{\mathcal{G}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(\frac{2(A_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{v_{{\mathcal{F}}}}A_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{v_{{\mathcal{G}}}})^{1/(v_{{\mathcal{F}}}+v_{{\mathcal{G}}})}}{\epsilon}\right)^{v_{{\mathcal{F}}}+v_{{\mathcal{G}}}},

which implies
{\mathcal{F}}+{\mathcal{G}}
is of VC type with characteristics
A^{\prime}=2(A_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{v_{{\mathcal{F}}}}A_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{v_{{\mathcal{G}}}})^{1/v^{\prime}}
and
v^{\prime}=v_{{\mathcal{F}}}+v_{{\mathcal{G}}}
.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Suppose that function class
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}:{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is pointwise measurable with envelope function
\mathrm{F}_{n}
and is of VC type with characteristics
(A_{n},v_{n})
, satisfying
A_{n}\geq e
and
v_{n}\geq 1
. Suppose that
\log(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\|f_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}/\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2})\lesssim\log n
and
\|\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim M_{n}
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}f-{\mathbb{P}}f\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]\lesssim\frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{M_{n}\cdot v_{n}\log(A_{n}\vee n)}{n}.

Proof.

For any constant sequence satisfying
\log a_{n}/b_{n}\lesssim\log n
, we have the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle J_{1}(a_{n}/b_{n},{\mathcal{F}},\mathrm{F})=

\displaystyle\ \int_{0}^{a_{n}/b_{n}}\sup_{Q}\left\{1+\log\mathsf{N}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{Q,2})\right\}^{1/2}{\mathrm{d}}\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \int_{0}^{a_{n}/b_{n}}\sqrt{1+v_{n}\log(A_{n}/\epsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \left(\int_{0}^{a_{n}/b_{n}}1^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\epsilon\right)^{1/2}\cdot\left(\int_{0}^{a_{n}/b_{n}}\{1+v_{n}\log(A_{n}/\epsilon)\}{\mathrm{d}}\epsilon\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sqrt{\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}}\cdot\left\{\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}(1+v_{n}\log A_{n})+v_{n}\left(-\epsilon\log\epsilon+\epsilon\right)\big|_{0}^{a_{n}/b_{n}}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sqrt{\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}}\cdot\left\{\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}(1+v_{n}\log A_{n})+v_{n}\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}\{\log(a_{n}/b_{n})+1\}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}a_{n}/b_{n})}\lesssim\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}\vee n)},

where the second inequality holds by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the penultimate inequality by
A_{n}\geq e
,
v_{n}\geq 1
, and the last by
\log a_{n}/b_{n}\lesssim\log n
.
Then, applying this result and Theorem 5.1 in
(
11
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}f-{\mathbb{P}}f\right\|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\right]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{J_{1}(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}/\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2},{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})\cdot\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle+\frac{J_{1}(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}/\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2},{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathrm{F}_{n})^{2}\cdot\|\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}}{n\cdot\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}^{2}/\|\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|f\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sqrt{v_{n}\log(A_{n}\vee n)}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{M_{n}\cdot v_{n}\log(A_{n}\vee n)}{n}.

∎

Lemma 14
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\{f:{\mathcal{O}}^{r}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\}
is of VC type with characteristics
(A_{n},v_{n})
and envelope
\mathrm{F}_{n}
, then for every
k=1,\ldots,r-1
,

(i)

\mathbb{P}^{r-k}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is also of VC type with characteristics
(4\sqrt{A_{n}},2v_{n})
and envelope
\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}
.

(ii)

Suppose further that
\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}f(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq m_{n}
and
\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
. Then,
\mathbb{P}^{r-k}{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is of VC type with characteristics
(4\sqrt{A_{n}}M_{n}/m_{n},2v_{n})
and envelope
m_{n}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}(\mathbb{P}^{r-k}{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\tau m_{n})\leq\left(\frac{4M_{n}\sqrt{A_{n}}}{m_{n}\tau}\right)^{2v_{n}},\ \text{for all}\ 0<\tau\leq 1.

Proof.

Lemma 5.4 of
Chen and Kato 2020
states that for any VC-type function class
{\mathcal{F}}

with characteristics
(A_{n},v_{n})
,
\mathbb{P}^{r-k}{\mathcal{F}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}(\mathbb{P}^{r-k}{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\epsilon\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{Q,2})\leq(4\sqrt{A_{n}}/\epsilon)^{2v_{n}},0<\forall\epsilon\leq 1,

which establishes part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, set

\varepsilon=\tau m_{n}/\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{Q,2}
in the display above. Then, since
\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{Q,2}\leq\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\mathsf{N}(\mathbb{P}^{r-k}{\mathcal{F}}_{n},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2},\tau m_{n})\leq\left(\frac{4\|\mathbb{P}^{r-k}\mathrm{F}_{n}\|_{Q,2}\sqrt{A_{n}}}{m_{n}\tau}\right)^{2v_{n}}\leq\left(\frac{4M_{n}\sqrt{A_{n}}}{m_{n}\tau}\right)^{2v_{n}}.

∎

Appendix S7
Proof of Results in Section
3.3

Before starting the proof, we first summarize some frequently used results. Under Assumptions
6
(iii) and
8
(i)–(iii), Lemma 16 of
(
15
)
implies the following uniform bound: for any function
f\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{T})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi[f\mid\bar{\phi}_{k}](\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\|f(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}.

(S7.1)

Recall that
\Pi[f\mid\bar{\phi}_{k}]
is the projection of
f
onto the linear span of basis
\bar{\phi}_{k}
, defined in Section
1.3
.

Because the proof carefully tracks changes in
\bm{\beta}
, we now make this dependence explicit by moving
\bm{\beta}
out of subscripts into argument parentheses. For notational simplicity, hereafter we sometimes write
\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\coloneqq\Gamma(\cdot,\cdot,\bm{\beta})
and denote
\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}\coloneqq\widehat{\Xi}(O_{1},O_{2};\bm{\beta})
if this causes no ambiguity.

S7.1
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof of Theorem
1
.

First, we will show, for any
\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}
,

\displaystyle\|\psi(\bm{\beta})\|\gtrsim\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|\wedge c

(S7.2)

for some constant
c
. Given (
S7.2
), we obtain
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|]\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}
, if we show that
\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}
with probability approaching one.

By Assumption
5
(ii), for any
\epsilon\leq c_{3}/2p
(
c_{3}
is as defined in Assumption
5
(ii)), there exists
\delta>0
such that for any
\bm{\beta}
satisfying
\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|\leq\delta
, we have
\|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta})-\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon
.
Then, for any
\bm{\beta}
satisfying
\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|\leq\delta
, since
\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})=0
, by Taylor expansion, we have

\displaystyle\psi(\bm{\beta})=\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{{\dagger}})(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}),

where
\bm{\beta}^{{\dagger}}
lies between
\bm{\beta}^{\ast}
and
\bm{\beta}
. Then, by Assumption
5
(iii), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{{\dagger}})(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|\geq

\displaystyle\ \|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|-\|\{\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{{\dagger}})-\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|-\epsilon p\cdot\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|-\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\cdot(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\|\geq\frac{c_{3}}{2}\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|.

For any
\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|\geq\delta
, by Assumption
4
,
\|\psi(\bm{\beta})\|\geq c
for some constant
c
. Hence, combining these two cases, we obtain (
S7.2
).

Next, we show
\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}
with probability approaching one. Since
\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\equiv 0
and
\psi(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})=0
, we decompose
\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|
as follows

\displaystyle\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|\leq

\displaystyle\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|+\|\bar{\psi}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|+\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|

\displaystyle+\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|+\|\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|.

Since
\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\equiv 0
by the definition of
\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
, we derive

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|=\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|\leq\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|+\|\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|.

Thus

\displaystyle\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\psi(\bm{\beta})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})\|+3\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})\|\eqqcolon 3I+3II.

Convergence Rate for
I
:

\displaystyle\psi(\bm{\beta})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\xi(X,T)\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta})\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\left\{\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta})-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X,T)\right\}+\xi(X,T)\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X,T)\right]

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\xi(X,T)-\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\right\}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)^{\top}\right]\Sigma_{k}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)\left\{b_{\bm{\beta}}(X,T)-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X,T)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\xi(X,T)-\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\right\}\cdot\left\{b_{\bm{\beta}}(X,T)-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X,T)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\xi(X,T)-\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\right\}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)^{\top}\right]\Sigma_{k}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)\left\{b_{\bm{\beta}}(X,T)-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X,T)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\Pi^{\bot}\left[\xi-\widehat{\xi}\mid\bar{\phi}_{k}\right](X,T)\cdot\Pi^{\bot}\left[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}\mid\bar{\phi}_{k}\right](X,T)\right]=\mathrm{TB}_{\psi,k}(\bm{\beta}).

(S7.3)

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle I\leq\

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\mathrm{TB}_{\psi,k}(\bm{\beta})\|\lesssim\|\Pi^{\perp}(\widehat{\xi}-\xi|\bar{\phi}_{k})\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\Pi^{\perp}[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{P},2}.

(S7.4)

Convergence Rate for
II
:

Recalling the definition of
\mathsf{IF}_{\bar{\psi}_{k}}^{(2)}
from Lemma
1
in Section
S3
, we consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\mathsf{IF}_{\bar{\psi}_{k}}^{(2)}-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}-{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}]\right)+{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}\left(\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}]\right).

(S7.5)

We will derive the convergence rate uniformly in
\bm{\beta}
for each term in (
S7.1
).
For the second term in (
S7.1
), consider the function class
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
defined as follows

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left\{h(O_{1},O_{2},\bm{\beta})=\widehat{\Xi}(O_{1},O_{2};\bm{\beta}):\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}\right\}.

(S7.6)

By Assumption
7
,
\{\Gamma(y,t,\bm{\beta}):\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}\}
is of VC type with characteristics
(A,v)
. By Lemma
12
,
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
is of VC type with bounded characteristics
(4A,v)
and envelope
\mathrm{H}_{1}(O_{1},O_{2})=\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\widehat{\Xi}(O_{1},O_{2};\bm{\beta})\right\|
. By Assumptions
6
and
7
, we have
\|\mathrm{H}_{1}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}\lesssim 1
. By Jensen’s inequality, we have
\|\mathbb{P}\mathrm{H}_{1}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\lesssim\|\mathrm{H}_{1}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}\lesssim 1
. Then, using Lemma
9
for function class
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}(\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}})-\mathbb{P}^{2}(\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}})\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

(S7.7)

To control the first term in (
S7.1
), we need to consider the function class

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}\coloneqq\left\{h(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3},\bm{\beta})=\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta}):\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}\right\}.

(S7.8)

Then, by Assumption
7
and Lemma
12
,
{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}
is of VC type with bounded characteristics
(4A,v)
and envelope
\mathrm{H}_{2,n}
, which is defined as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{2,n}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\widehat{\xi}(X_{1},T_{1})\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{1},T_{1})-\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k_{x}}(X_{1})\otimes\bar{{\mathsf{w}}}_{k_{t}}(T_{3})\right\}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta})-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X_{2},T_{2})\right\|.

Using Assumptions
6
,
7
and
8
, we have
\|\mathbb{P}^{2}\mathrm{H}_{2,n}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|\mathrm{H}_{2,n}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\zeta(k)^{2}\lesssim k
, and

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{H}_{2,n}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}=

\displaystyle\ \left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\mathrm{H}_{2,n}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3})\|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \left\{{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta})-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X_{2},T_{2})\right\|^{2}\bigg]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \zeta(k)\lesssim\sqrt{k}.

For any
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}
,
\mathbb{P}^{2}h
can be written explicitly as

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}^{2}h(O)=&\ \frac{1}{3}\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\Pi[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X,T)-\frac{1}{3}\int_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\Pi[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X,t)\mathsf{p}_{T}(t){\mathrm{d}}t\\
&+\frac{1}{3}\Pi[\widehat{\xi}-\xi|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X,T)\left(\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\int_{x\in{\mathcal{X}}}\Pi[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](x,T)\mathsf{p}_{X}(x){\mathrm{d}}x,\end{split}

(S7.9)

which is a combination of projection terms. By Assumptions
6
and
7
, we have
\|\xi(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
,
\|\widehat{\xi}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
,
\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\Gamma(\cdot,\cdot,\bm{\beta})\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, and
\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
.
Thus, using the result in (
S7.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2}h(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\Pi[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{\infty}+\|\Pi[\widehat{\xi}-\xi|\bar{\phi}_{k}](\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|(b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}})(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}+\|(\widehat{\xi}-\xi)(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1.

This also implies
\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2}h\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\lesssim 1
.
Hence, applying Lemma
10
to
{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}\right)-\mathbb{P}^{3}\left(\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}\right)\right\|\right]\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{k}\log n}{n}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}.

(S7.10)

Hence, by (
S7.1
), (
S7.7
), (
S7.10
), we have

\displaystyle II=\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})\right\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{k}\log n}{n}\vee\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(S7.11)

Combining (
S7.4
) and (
S7.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\right\|=

\displaystyle\ O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{k}\log n}{n}\vee\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}+\|\Pi^{\perp}(\widehat{\xi}-\xi|\bar{\phi}_{k})\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\Pi^{\perp}[b_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ O_{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}),

which completes the proof.

∎

S7.2
Proof of Theorem
3

Proof of Theorem
3
.

By construction, we have
\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})=0
and
\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})=0
. Consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle 0=\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})]+{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]

\displaystyle+\ {\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]+{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})]\right\|+\left\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]\right\|

\displaystyle\ +\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]\|+\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|.

Using the condition that for some constant
c>0
,
\lVert{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}_{1})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}_{2})]\rVert\geq c\|\bm{\beta}_{1}-\bm{\beta}_{2}\|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \ \sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})]\right\|+\left\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\right\|.

Conditioning on the nuisance sample, the analysis of
\ \sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\lVert\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})]\rVert
closely parallels that of the term
\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\|\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})]\|
, so both terms attain the same convergence rate. Thus, by the result in (
S7.11
), we have, with probability approaching one,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}}\left\|\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta})]\right\|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{k}\log n}{n}\vee\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}.

By (
S7.1
), we have
{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})=\mathrm{TB}_{\psi,k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})
. Hence
\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|=\|\mathrm{TB}_{\psi,k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}
.
It remains to control the term
{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]
.

\displaystyle\ \left\|{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left\|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\xi(X,T)-\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\right\}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)^{\top}\right]\{\widehat{\Sigma}_{k}^{-1}-\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)\left\{b_{\bm{\beta}^{*}}(X,T)-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}(X,T)\right\}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\xi-\widehat{\xi}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\|b_{\bm{\beta}^{*}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{k}-\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Combining all terms, we obtain the desired bound:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\|]\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}+\|\xi-\widehat{\xi}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\|b_{\bm{\beta}^{*}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{k}-\Sigma_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

By Assumption
6
(ii), we have ∥b_
β
^* -

b

_

∥_

\displaystyle\|\xi-\widehat{\xi}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\|b_{\bm{\beta}^{*}}-\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}_{\mathsf{init}}}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\cdot\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{k}-\Sigma_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o(n^{-1/2})\leq r_{n,\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}},

which implies
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|]\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}
.

S7.3
Proof of Theorem
2

The matrices
V_{1}(\bm{\beta})
and
V_{2}(\bm{\beta})
that appear in
Theorem
2
are defined by

\displaystyle V_{1}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{n}{n+k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{2\pi_{1}\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}+3\pi_{1}\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right\}^{\otimes 2}\right]

(S7.12)

\displaystyle\text{ and }V_{2}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{k+n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\pi_{2}\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}+3\pi_{2}\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right\}^{\otimes 2}\right].

(S7.13)

Proof of Theorem
2
.

By Assumption
5
,
\psi(\bm{\beta})
is differentiable with respect to
\bm{\beta}
. Using Mean Value Theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle 0=\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})=\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})+\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{{\dagger}})\cdot(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}),

where
\bm{\beta}^{{\dagger}}
lies between
\bm{\beta}^{*}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
. Because
\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta})
is continuous in
\bm{\beta}
at
\bm{\beta}^{*}
, and
\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, we have

\displaystyle{n}/{\sqrt{k+n}}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{*})=-\left[\nabla_{\bm{\beta}}\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})\right]^{-1}\cdot{n}/{\sqrt{k+n}}\cdot\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(S7.14)

Since
0=\psi(\bm{\beta}^{*})
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})=0
, we have the following decomposition

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\left\{\psi(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\right\}

(S7.15)

\displaystyle+\frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\left\{\bar{\psi}_{k}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\left(\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})-\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\right)\right\}

(S7.16)

\displaystyle-\frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\left\{\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\right\}.

(S7.17)

By (
S7.1
), (
11
) and
\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})
, the right-hand side of (
S7.15
) is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. By (
11
) and Lemma
16
, (
S7.17
) converges to a normal distribution. Combining this and (
S7.14
), we obtain the desired distribution of
\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
as stated in Theorem
2
. To complete the proof, it remains to show (
S7.16
), which is a
U
-process indexed by
\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
, is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Since we have shown in Section
S7.1
that
\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}
with probability
1-o(1)
, it remains to show that, conditioned on this event, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\cdot\left\|\left\{\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right\}-\left\{\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})-\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\right\}\right\|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=o(1).

(S7.18)

Consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle\left\{\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})\right\}-\left\{\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})-\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\right\}

(S7.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})-\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})-\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})]\right)+{\mathbb{U}}_{n,2}\left(\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}-{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}]\right).

First, we define the function class

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{1,\delta}\coloneqq\bigg\{h(O,\bm{\beta})=\ \widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{\Xi}_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}=\ \widehat{\xi}(X,T)[\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta}^{*})]:\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}},\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}\bigg\}.

(S7.20)

By Assumption
7
and Lemma
12
, the function class
{\mathcal{H}}_{1,\delta}
of VC type with bounded characteristics
(8A,v)
and envelope
\mathrm{H}_{1,\delta}(O)\coloneqq\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\|\widehat{\xi}(X,T)[\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta}^{*})]\|
. Since
\|\widehat{\xi}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
under Assumption
6
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{H}_{1,\delta}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{2},2}\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}^{1/2}\bigg[\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\|\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta}^{*})\|^{2}\bigg]\lesssim(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}},

where the last inequality holds by Assumptions
7
.
Applying Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 2023
to
{\mathcal{H}}_{1,\delta}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,1}(h)-\mathbb{P}^{2}(h)\right\|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1,\delta}}\right]\lesssim\frac{J_{1}(1,{\mathcal{H}}_{1,\delta},\mathrm{H}_{1,\delta})\cdot\|\mathrm{H}_{1,\delta}\|_{\mathbb{P},2}}{\sqrt{n}}\lesssim\frac{(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(S7.21)

Similarly, we define the following function class

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n,\delta}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}h(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3},\bm{\beta})=\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})-\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{*}):\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}},\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}\end{array}\right\}.

Using arguments analogous to those used for
{\mathcal{H}}_{1,\delta}
, the function class is also VC type with bounded characteristics, and its envelope function
\mathrm{H}_{2,n,\delta}
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{2,n,\delta}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \left|\left\{\widehat{\xi}(X_{1},T_{1})\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{1},T_{1})-\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k_{x}}(X_{1})\otimes\bar{{\mathsf{w}}}_{k_{t}}(T_{3})\right\}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\left\|\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta}^{*})\right\|.

Note that,
\|\xi(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
and
\|\widehat{\xi}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
under Assumption
6
. Using Assumptions
7
and
8
, we have
\|\mathbb{P}^{2}\mathrm{H}_{2,n,\delta}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|\mathrm{H}_{2,n,\delta}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\zeta(k)^{2}\lesssim k
and

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\mathrm{H}_{2,n,\delta}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\leq\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\left\|\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta}^{*})\right\|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \zeta(k)^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\left\|\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta}^{*})\right\|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ k\cdot(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{2\alpha_{0}},

which implies
\|\mathrm{H}_{2,n,\delta}\|_{\mathbb{P}^{3},2}\lesssim\sqrt{k}\cdot(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}
.

For any
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n,\delta}
, the explicit expression of
\mathbb{P}^{2}h
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{2}h(O)=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{3}\widehat{\xi}(X,T)\Pi[\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}}-\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X,T)-\frac{1}{3}\int_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\Pi[\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}}-\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X,t)\mathsf{p}_{T}(t){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}\Pi[\widehat{\xi}-\xi|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X,T)\left(\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}}-\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\int_{x\in{\mathcal{X}}}\Pi[\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}}-\Gamma_{\bm{\beta}^{\ast}}|\bar{\phi}_{k}](x,T)\mathsf{p}_{X}(x){\mathrm{d}}x.

Since
\|\xi(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
,
\|\widehat{\xi}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
and projections do not increase length, using Jensen’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n,\delta}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2}h\|_{\mathbb{P},2}\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}^{1/2}\bigg[\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\|\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta})-\Gamma(Y,T,\bm{\beta}^{*})\|^{2}\bigg]\lesssim(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}},

where the last inequality holds by Assumption
7
.
By Assumptions
6
,
7
, and (
S7.1
), we have sup_
β
‖ Π[Γ_
β
—
¯

ϕ

_k] (⋅, ⋅)‖_∞ ≤sup_
β
‖ Γ_
β
(⋅,⋅) ‖_∞ ≲1,
and
‖ Π[
^

ξ

-
ξ
—
¯

ϕ

_k] (⋅,⋅) ‖_∞ ≲∥(
^

ξ

-
ξ
) (⋅,⋅)∥_∞ ≲1 ,
which implies
\sup_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n,\delta}}\|\mathbb{P}^{2}h(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
.
Hence, applying Lemma
10
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}(h)-\mathbb{P}^{3}h\right\|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{2,n,\delta}}\right]\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{k}\cdot(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}\log n+\log n}{n}+\ \frac{(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(S7.23)

Hence, combining (
S7.19
), (
S7.21
), and (
S7.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\ast}\|\lesssim\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}}}\frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\cdot\left\|\left(\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta})-\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right)-\left(\bar{\psi}_{k}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})-\widetilde{\psi}_{k}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta}^{\ast})\right)\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \frac{n}{\sqrt{k+n}}\cdot\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}++\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n}\right]\cdot(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}\log n

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{k+n}}+\sqrt{\frac{k}{k+n}}\right)\cdot(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}\log n=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where the last equality holds since we assume
(\widetilde{r}_{n,\bm{\beta}})^{\alpha_{0}}\log n\rightarrow 0
. Hence, we have shown that (
S7.18
) is
o(1)
, which completes the proof.
∎

S7.4
Technical Lemmas for Results in Section
3.3

Lemma 15
.

Suppose that Assumptions
6
–
8
hold.
Then, for a given
\bm{\beta}\in{\mathcal{B}}
, conditioning on the nuisance sample,

\left\|\mathrm{cov}\left[{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{k}{n^{2}}\right),

where
\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})
is defined in Lemma
1
.

Proof.

Taking a unit-norm vector
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
(
\|v\|=1
), we need to show

\displaystyle v^{\top}\mathrm{cov}\left[{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right)\right]v=\mathrm{var}\left[{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(v^{\top}\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right)\right]=O\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{k}{n^{2}}\right).

By using Lemma 15 in
(
15
)
,

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{var}\left[{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left(v^{\top}\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right)\right]=\mathrm{var}\left[{\mathbb{U}}_{n,3}\left\{S_{3}\left(v^{\top}\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(\bm{\beta})\right)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{1}{n^{j}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_{3}\left(v^{\top}\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta})\right)\mid O_{1},\cdots,O_{j}\right]\right\}^{2}\right]\eqqcolon\sum_{j=1}^{3}\zeta_{j},

where
S_{3}
is the symmetrization operator defined in Section
S6.1
.
For
\zeta_{1}
, first note that

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta})^{\top}v\mid O_{1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left\{\widehat{\xi}(X_{1},T_{1})\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{1},T_{1})-\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k_{x}}(X_{1})\otimes{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bar{{\mathsf{w}}}_{k_{t}}(T)\right]\right\}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bar{\phi}_{k}(X,T)\left\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}(X,T)-b_{\bm{\beta}}(X,T)\right\}^{\top}v\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\xi}(X_{1},T_{1})\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},T_{1})-\int_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},t)\mathsf{p}_{T}(t){\mathrm{d}}t.

Then

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\widehat{\xi}(X_{1},T_{1})\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},T_{1})\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},T_{1})\right|^{2}\right]\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v\right|^{2}\right]\lesssim 1,

where the second inequality follows by the
L_{2}
-norm contraction property of
L_{2}
-projection and the last inequality follows by Assumption
6
. Similarly, by Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\int_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},t)\mathsf{p}_{T}(t){\mathrm{d}}t\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{T}}}\left|\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](x,t)\right|^{2}\mathsf{p}_{T}(t)\mathsf{p}_{X}(x){\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\xi(X_{1},T_{1})\left|\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},T_{1})\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\Pi[\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}-b_{\bm{\beta}}\}^{\top}v|\bar{\phi}_{k}](X_{1},T_{1})\right|^{2}\right]\lesssim 1.

Hence,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta})^{\top}v\mid O_{1}]\right\}^{2}\right]=O(1).

By symmetry, we also have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta})^{\top}v\mid O_{2}]\right\}^{2}\right]=O(1),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta})^{\top}v\mid O_{3}]\right\}^{2}\right]=O(1).

Taking these together implies
\zeta_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}
.

For
\zeta_{2}
, first note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{IF}_{\mathrm{B}_{\psi,k}}^{(2)}(O_{1},O_{2},O_{3};\bm{\beta})^{\top}v\mid O_{1},O_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{\widehat{\xi}(X_{1},T_{1})\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{1},T_{1})-\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{k_{x}}(X_{1})\otimes\int_{{\mathcal{T}}}\bar{{\mathsf{w}}}_{k_{t}}(t)\mathsf{p}_{T}(t){\mathrm{d}}t\big\}^{\top}\Sigma_{k}^{-1}\bar{\phi}_{k}(X_{2},T_{2})\left\{\widehat{b}_{\bm{\beta}}(X_{2},T_{2})-\Gamma(Y_{2},T_{2},\bm{\beta})\right\}^{\top}v.

By Assumptions
6
–
8
, we have
